    11	
    12	pub(super) fn observe(engine:&mut Engine,p:&Program,viewer:&str,args:BTreeMap<String,Value>)->Result<serde_json::Value,String>{
    13	    let context=Context::new(viewer,args).at(NOW);
    14	    if p.kind==ProgramKind::Query {
    15	        match engine.query(&p.name,context) {
    16	            Ok(q) if q.unavailable || !q.complete => Err("incomplete/metered query is outside logical payload comparison".into()),
    17	            Ok(q)=>Ok(serde_json::json!({"data":q.data})),
    18	            Err(e) if e.rule.is_some() && matches!(e.family.as_str(),"rule"|"constraint") => Ok(serde_json::json!({"refusal":{"code":e.code,"message":e.message}})),
    19	            Err(e)=>Err(format!("query error is not an admitted observation: {e}")),
    20	        }
    21	    }else{
    22	        match engine.mutate(&p.name,None,context).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.state {
    23	            snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{result,..}=>Ok(serde_json::json!({"sent":result})),
    24	            snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{why,current}=>{
    25	                // E_RULE can also mask authorization-budget exhaustion. Only
    26	                // an authored require/constraint carries this rule marker;
    27	                // bound/storage/schema/ambiguous engine refusals cannot be a
    28	                // logical counterexample in our resource-excluding contract.
    29	                if why.rule.is_none()||!matches!(why.family.as_str(),"rule"|"constraint") {
    30	                    return Err(format!("resource/engine or ambiguous mutation refusal {} is outside paired logical comparison",why.code));
    31	                }
    32	                Ok(serde_json::json!({"refusal":{"code":why.code,"message":why.message},"current":current}))
    33	            },
    34	        }
    35	    }
    36	}
    37	
    38	pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>){
    39	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c|c=="id")&&sites.is_none_or(|s|s.contains(&i.site.to_string()))) {
    40	        if inv.policy.is_none(){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired observation needs complete bound policy dependencies; unsupported policy AST is not dependency-free",inv.table));continue;}
    41	        if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()){continue;}
    42	        // Closed transitive observer set: include maintained targets, inherited
    43	        // readers and every table whose rule probes an affected population.
    44	        // An operation mentioning none of them has no logical input changed
    45	        // by this pair. This prunes experiments, never proves an obligation.
    46	        let mut observers=BTreeSet::from([inv.table.clone()]);
    47	        loop {
    48	            let before=observers.len();
    49	            for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if observers.contains(&m.source){
    50	                observers.insert(m.target.clone());
    51	                if let Some(owner)=&m.private{observers.insert(owner.table.clone());}
    52	            }}
    53	            for (name,t) in &bound.schema.tables {
    54	                if t.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|observers.contains(&p.parent))
    55	                    || [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).iter().any(|d|observers.contains(d))) {
    56	                    observers.insert(name.clone());
    57	                }
    58	            }
    59	            if observers.len()==before{break;}
    60	        }
    61	        // Removing an ordinary row must not manufacture an invalid reference
    62	        // graph. More general paired deletions need a checked closure.
    63	        if bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Ref{table} if table==&inv.table))) {
    64	            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired absence needs a referentially valid world closure",inv.table));continue;
    65	        }
    66	        for variant in 0..8 {
    67	            if !out.reserve(1){return;}
    68	            let Ok(present)=snapshot(bound,inv,variant)else{continue;};
    69	            if present.keys().any(|name|bound.schema.tables[name].columns.values().any(C::contains_asset)) {
    70	                out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired fixture asset identities are not coupled",inv.table));continue;
    71	            }
    72	            let Some(row)=present.get(&inv.table).and_then(|rs|rs.first()).cloned()else{continue;};
    73	            let prepared=(||->Result<Value,String>{
    74	                let e=engine(bound,&present,program("$security.pair.image",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))?;
    75	                let actual=readback(&e)?;
    76	                actual.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).cloned().ok_or_else(||"protected paired row is absent after validation".into())
    77	            })();
    78	            let row=prepared.as_ref().unwrap_or(&row);
    79	            let mut absent=present.clone();absent.remove(&inv.table);
    80	            // Final-exists validation is a pure judgment of this fixed seeded
    81	            // world, before operation/viewer admission. Retain its rejection
    82	            // across observers, never cache execution/resource/policy results.
    83	            let invalid=std::cell::RefCell::new([None::<String>,None]);
    84	            for viewer in [VIEWER,OTHER,"account:security-no-role","service:security"] {
    85	                for bp in &bound.programs {
    86	                    let p=&bp.program;
    87	                    if !matches!(p.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.native {continue;}
    88	                    let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
    89	                    let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
    90	                    if writes.iter().any(|(table,kind)|table==&inv.table||deps.contains(table)||*kind!="update")
    91	                        || !writes.is_empty() && (!bound.schema.maintains.is_empty() || bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|!t.sequences.is_empty()||!t.on_delete.is_empty())) {
    92	                        out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired mutation lacks stable policy/implicit-write and deterministic-id reduction",p.name));continue;
    93	                    }
    94	                    let json=serde_json::to_value(p).unwrap();
    95	                    if !observers.iter().any(|table|super::super::audit::mentions(&json,table)){continue;}
    96	                    // Fresh generated ids and opaque viewer/work contexts are
    97	                    // not repeatable functions of these paired inputs.
    98	                    fn opaque(v:&serde_json::Value)->bool {match v{
    99	                        serde_json::Value::String(s)=>s=="NewId"||s=="JobAs",
   100	                        serde_json::Value::Object(m)=>m.keys().any(|k|matches!(k.as_str(),"Emit"|"JobAs"|"JobQuery"))||m.values().any(opaque),
   101	                        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(opaque),_=>false,
   102	                    }}
   103	                    if opaque(&json){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated-id/opaque effects excluded from paired determinism",p.name));continue;}
   104	                    let cases=histories::fixture_cases(p,&present,&[VIEWER.into(),OTHER.into()],out);
   105	                    if cases.len()>32{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired observer arguments capped at 32; remaining combinations not covered",p.name));}
   106	                    for args in cases.into_iter().take(32){
   107	                    if !out.reserve(12){return;}
   108	                        if out.paired_observer_calls+4>8192{out.unsupported.insert("paired observers capped at 8192 call reservations; remaining worlds not covered".into());return;}
   109	                    out.paired_observer_calls+=4;
   110	                    let execute=|seeds:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>|->Result<(serde_json::Value,BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>),String>{
   111	                        if let Err(why)=&prepared{return Err(why.clone());}
   112	
   113	                        let slot=usize::from(std::ptr::eq(seeds,&absent));
   114	                        if let Some(why)=&invalid.borrow()[slot]{return Err(why.clone());}
   115	                        let mut e=match engine(bound,seeds,program("$security.pair",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){
   116	                            Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{
   117	                                if why.ends_with(": trusted seed did not establish final-exists constraint"){invalid.borrow_mut()[slot]=Some(why.clone());}
   118	                                return Err(why);
   119	                            },
   120	                        };
   121	                        let actual=readback(&e)?;
   122	                        // Judge the hypothetical protected row in each complete
   123	                        // committed world, before any operation can change roles.
   124	                        let focal=actual.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||row.clone());
   125	                        if policy_in(bound,inv,&actual,viewer,None,&focal,&mut e)!=Ok(false){return Err("paired policy is not definitely false in both worlds".into());}
   126	                        observe(&mut e,p,viewer,args.clone()).map(|value|(value,actual))
   127	                    };
   128	                    let ((a,rows),(b,absent_rows))=match (execute(&present),execute(&absent)) {
   129	                        (Ok(a),Ok(b))=>(a,b),
   130	                        (Err(e),_)|(_,Err(e))=>{
   131	                            if !e.starts_with("paired policy is not definitely false") {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.name));}
   132	                            continue;
   133	                        },
   134	                    };
   135	                    out.attempted+=2;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=2;
   136	                    if a==b {continue;}
   137	                    // The query order is engine-defined. Recreate both worlds
   138	                    // to decline store-scoped cursors or unstable outputs.
   139	                    if !matches!((execute(&present),execute(&absent)),(Ok((aa,_)),Ok((bb,_))) if aa==a&&bb==b){
   140	                        out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired observation is not repeatable",p.name));continue;
   141	                    }
   142	                    out.violations.push(Counterexample {reachability:Reachability::SyntheticOperation,history:Vec::new(),invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:args.clone(),viewer:viewer.into(),
   143	                        operation:p.name.clone(),effect:"read observation".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:rows,
   144	                        rule_path:"paired present/absent engine executions differ while the declared privacy policy is false in both".into(),
   145	                        scope:"schema-valid seed-ingress paired worlds; authenticated logical data/refusal only; no application reachability claim".into(),
   146	                        observations:Some(Observations{present:a,absent:b,absent_rows})});
   147	                    break;
   148	                    }
   149	                    if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.observations.is_some()){break;}
   150	                }
   151	                if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.observations.is_some()){break;}
   152	            }
   153	            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.observations.is_some()){break;}
   154	        }
   155	    }
   156	    out.unsupported.insert("paired observations exclude wire sequences/receipts, physical timing and resource/meter differences; HTTP and device histories require separate qualification".into());
   157	}
     1	//! Concrete, isolated challenges; finite campaigns never establish proof.

    21	#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize,PartialEq,Eq)]
    22	#[serde(rename_all="kebab-case")]
    23	pub enum Reachability { AuthoredFixtureTrace, GeneratedFixtureTrace, TrustedSeedOnly, SyntheticOperation }
    24	impl Reachability {
    25	    pub fn is_reachable(&self)->bool {matches!(self,Self::AuthoredFixtureTrace|Self::GeneratedFixtureTrace)}

    27	#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize)]
    28	pub struct Counterexample {pub reachability:Reachability,pub invariant_site:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub viewer:String,pub operation:String,pub effect:String,pub row:Value,pub old:Option<Value>,pub supporting_rows:BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,pub rule_path:String,pub scope:String, pub observations:Option<paired::Observations>,pub history:Vec<histories::Step>}
    29	#[derive(Clone,Debug,Default,Serialize)]
    30	pub struct Campaign {#[serde(skip)] pub history_limit:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub generated_fixture:bool,#[serde(skip)] pub work_remaining:Option<usize>,#[serde(skip)] pub deadline:Option<std::time::Instant>,pub paired_observer_calls:usize,pub history_observer_calls:usize,pub column_observer_calls:usize,pub operations:BTreeMap<String,usize>,pub sequences:usize,pub attempted:usize,pub admitted:usize,pub refused:usize,pub violations:Vec<Counterexample>,pub boundary_failures:Vec<String>,pub unsupported:BTreeSet<String>}
    31	impl Campaign {
    32	    pub fn bounded(milliseconds:u64)->Self {Self{work_remaining:Some(512),deadline:Some(std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_millis(milliseconds)),..Self::default()}}
    33	    pub(super) fn reserve(&mut self,cost:usize)->bool {
    34	        if self.exhausted(){return false;}
    35	        if let Some(remaining)=&mut self.work_remaining {
    36	            if *remaining<cost{*remaining=0;self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return false;}
    37	            *remaining-=cost;
    38	        }true
    39	    }
    40	    pub(super) fn exhausted(&mut self)->bool {
    41	        if self.work_remaining==Some(0){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return true;}
    42	        if self.deadline.is_some_and(|deadline|std::time::Instant::now()>=deadline){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration time budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());true}else{false}
    43	    }
    44	}
    45	fn id(table:&str)->Value {Value::Id(format!("security-{table}"))}
    46	fn sample(kind:&C,variant:usize)->Option<Value>{Some(match kind{
    47	    C::Optional(inner)=>if variant&4!=0{sample(inner,variant).unwrap_or(Value::Null)}else{Value::Null},C::Id|C::Principal=>Value::Id(if variant&1==0{VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into()),
    48	    C::Ref{table}=>id(table),C::Text{max}=>Value::String("x".repeat((*max).min(20) as usize)),
    49	    C::Enum{variants}=>Value::String(variants.get(variant%variants.len())?.clone()),
    50	    C::Int|C::Decimal{..}|C::Money{..}=>Value::Int(1),C::IntRange{min,max}=>Value::Int(1i64.clamp(*min,*max)),
    51	    C::Bool=>Value::Bool((variant>>2)&1==0),C::Time=>Value::Int(NOW-1000),C::Json{..}=>Value::Null,C::Bytes{..}=>Value::Bytes(Vec::new()),
    52	    C::List{..}=>Value::Array(Vec::new()),C::ListRange{of,min,..}=>Value::Array((0..*min).map(|_|sample(of,variant)).collect::<Option<_>>()?),
    53	    C::Image{..}=>Value::String("$security:image".into()),C::File{..}=>Value::String("$security:file".into()),C::Video{..}=>return None,
    54	})}
    55	fn needed(schema:&Schema,name:&str,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){
    56	    if !out.insert(name.into()){return;}let Some(t)=schema.table(name)else{return};
    57	    for m in schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.target==name){needed(schema,&m.source,out);}
    58	    for k in t.columns.values(){if let C::Ref{table}=k.required(){needed(schema,table,out);}}
    59	    for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){needed(schema,&dep,out);}}
    60	}
    61	fn snapshot(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    62	    snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,false)
    63	}
    64	fn snapshot_mode(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize,timed:bool)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    65	    let mut names=BTreeSet::new();needed(&bound.schema,&inv.table,&mut names);
    66	    if let Some(policy)=&inv.policy{for dep in kernel::dependencies(policy){needed(&bound.schema,&dep,&mut names);}}
    67	    if let Some(mut syntax)=inv.syntax.clone(){super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut syntax,&mut |e| {
    68	        if let ast::Expr::Read(r)|ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=e {needed(&bound.schema,&r.table,&mut names);}
    69	    });}
    70	    if let Some(op)=&inv.operation && let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op){for kind in p.program.args.values(){if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind{needed(&bound.schema,table,&mut names);}}}
    71	    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
    72	    let mut enum_case=variant;
    73	    for name in names {
    74	        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&name];
    75	        if bound.schema.maintain_for(&name).is_some(){continue;}
    76	        let mut fields=BTreeMap::new();fields.insert("id".into(),id(&name));
    77	        let mut principal_index=0usize;
    78	        for (n,k) in &t.columns{
    79	            if n=="id"||t.engine_field(n)||t.maintained.contains_key(n){continue;}
    80	            let sample_variant=if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){let n=enum_case % variants.len();enum_case/=variants.len();n}else if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal){let n=((variant>>((if name==inv.table{0}else{3})+principal_index.min(5)))&1)|(variant&4);principal_index+=1;n}else{variant};
    81	            let value=sample(k,sample_variant)
    82	                .ok_or_else(||format!("{name}.{n}: asset ledger/required sample is not available"))?;
    83	            fields.insert(n.clone(),value);
    84	        }
    85	        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
    86	            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
    87	        }}}
    88	        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
    89	            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
    90	        // Named primary keys used by role probes must select the challenged
    91	        // viewer; changing the enum represents a current-role change while
    92	        // derived assignment/membership rows remain present.
    93	        if name!=inv.table {for (i,(n,k)) in t.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k,C::Principal)).enumerate(){fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Id(if variant & (1 << (i+3)) == 0 {VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into())); let _=k;}}
    94	        out.insert(name,vec![Value::Row(fields.into())]);
    95	    }
    96	    Ok(out)
    97	}
    98	struct Snapshot<'a>{schema:&'a Schema,rows:&'a BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>}
    99	impl Probe for Snapshot<'_>{
   100	    fn exists(&mut self,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[Value],equalities:&[(String,Value)])->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
   101	        let t=&self.schema.tables[table];let Some(idx)=t.index(index)else{return Ok(false)};
   102	        if idx.components.len()!=key.len(){return Ok(false);}
   103	        if idx.unique&&idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column().is_some_and(|n|t.columns.get(n).is_some_and(C::is_optional)))&&key.contains(&Value::Null){return Ok(false);}
   104	        Ok(self.rows.get(table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|live(t,r)).any(|r|idx.components.iter().zip(key).all(|(c,v)|c.column().is_some_and(|n|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v)))&&equalities.iter().all(|(n,v)|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v))))
   105	    }
   106	    fn created(&mut self,_:&str,_:&str,_:&[Value])->bool{false}
   107	
   108	}
   109	fn live(t:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,row:&Value)->bool{
   110	    !t.expired(row,NOW)&&t.not_before.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|match row.field(c){Ok(Value::Int(n))=>*n<=NOW,Ok(Value::Null)=>true,_=>false})
   111	}
   112	fn policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value)->Result<bool,String>{
   113	
   114	    let Some(p)=inv.policy.as_ref() else{return inv.syntax.as_ref().and_then(|e|super::concrete::evaluate(e,row,rows,&bound.schema,viewer)).ok_or_else(||"concrete policy expression is unknown/unsupported (including clock and typed literal coercions)".into());};
   115	
   116	    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
   117	    if p.has_reference(){return Err("reference intent requires authorized engine policy replay".into());}
   118	    rules::evaluate(p,viewer,old,&image,false,&mut Snapshot{schema:&bound.schema,rows}).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
   119	}
   120	fn policy_in(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<bool,String>{
   121	    let Some(p)=&inv.policy else{return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);};
   122	    if !p.has_reference(){return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);}
   123	    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
   124	    match engine.fixture_policy(p,viewer,old,&image,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?{
   125	        rules::Verdict::Readable=>Ok(true),rules::Verdict::Denied=>Ok(false),rules::Verdict::Unknown=>Err("policy reference/coverage judgment is unknown; never a refutation".into()),
   126	    }
   127	}
   128	fn program(name:&str,kind:ProgramKind,body:Vec<Stmt>)->Program{Program{name:name.into(),kind,args:BTreeMap::new(),body,read_time_granularity_ms:None,returned_shapes:BTreeMap::new(),native:false}}
   129	fn engine(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probe:Program)->Result<Engine,String>{
   130	    engine_with(bound,rows,vec![probe])
   131	}
   132	fn engine_with(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
   133	    engine_on_store(bound,rows,probes,Store::memory().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?)
   134	}
   135	fn hydrate(engine:&mut Engine,kind:&C,value:&Value)->Result<Value,String>{
   375	        (ir::ArgType::Optional(inner),v) if *v!=Value::Null=>one(e,inner,v,viewer)?,
   376	        (ir::ArgType::List{of:Some(inner),..}|ir::ArgType::ListRange{of:Some(inner),..},Value::Array(xs))=>Value::Array(xs.iter().map(|v|one(e,inner,v,viewer)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
   377	        (ir::ArgType::Image{..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:image"=>e.store_asset(viewer,&PNG,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.column_value(),
   378	        (ir::ArgType::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(e,viewer,types)?,
   379	        _=>v.clone(),
   380	    })}
   381	    values.iter().map(|(n,v)|one(engine,&args[n],v,viewer).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
   382	}
   383	
   384	/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
   385	/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
   386	pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
   387	    for (name,_) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_some()){campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("ephemeral {name}: generated memory populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain uncovered; durable campaigns use isolated hub copies"));}
   388	    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
   389	    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
   390	    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
   391	        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
   392	        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
   393	    }
   394	    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
   395	    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
   396	        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
   397	        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
   398	    }
   399	    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
   400	}
   401	/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
   402	/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
   403	/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
   404	pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
   405	    let first=campaign.violations.len();
   406	    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
   407	    column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
   408	    if !campaign.generated_fixture {trusted_reads(bound,fixture,campaign);}
   409	    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
   410	    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
   411	        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
   412	    }}
   413	}
   414	pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
   415	    fixture_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
   416	    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
   417	    let fixtures=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
   418	    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());
   419	    if fixtures.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{campaign.unsupported.insert("operation fixture matrix exceeds 1000 rows; provide a focused security fixture".into());return;}
   420	    let mut viewers=histories::viewers(bound);
   421	    for (table,rows) in &fixtures{for row in rows{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!viewers.contains(p){viewers.push(p.clone());}}}}
   422	    let eligible=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)&&!p.program.native).count();
   423	    let quota=(8192/eligible.max(1)).clamp(1,512);let mut remaining=8192usize;
   424	    for p in &bound.programs{
   425	        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}
   426	        if bound.schema.operations.get(&p.program.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral){campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: ephemeral mutation history is not covered by the durable campaign",p.program.name));continue;}
   427	        let target=bound.schema.visible_tables().find(|(t,_)|super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t)).or_else(||bound.schema.visible_tables().next()).map(|(t,_)|t.clone());
   428	        let Some(target)=target else{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no table population for generated arguments",p.program.name));continue;};
   429	        let dummy=Invariant{table:target,target:"read".into(),column:None,predicate:"allow".into(),site:crate::Site::default(),policy:Some(RuleExpr::Allow),operation:Some(p.program.name.clone()),requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None};
   430	        let cases=histories::fixture_cases(&p.program,&fixtures,&viewers,campaign);
   431	        let mut exercised=0;
   432	        'cases:for (variant,mut args) in cases.into_iter().enumerate(){
   433	            let rows=if has_fixture{fixtures.clone()}else{match snapshot(bound,&dummy,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));break;}}};
   434	            if !has_fixture{for (arg,kind) in &p.program.args {if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind&&let Some(row)=rows.get(table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())&&let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(arg.clone(),id.clone());}}}
   435	            for viewer in &viewers{
   436	                if exercised==quota||remaining==0{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: operation matrix capped at {quota} calls to share the 8192-call budget across every operation; remaining arguments/viewers not covered",p.program.name));break 'cases;}
   437	                exercised+=1;remaining-=1;
   438	                let mut engine=match if has_fixture {fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())}else{engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.matrix",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))}{Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));continue;}};
   439	                let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut engine,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}",p.program.name));continue;}};
   440	                campaign.attempted+=1;*campaign.operations.entry(p.program.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
   441	                if p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query{
   442	                    match engine.query(&p.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(_)=>{},Err(e) if e.code=="E_INPUT"=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated arguments rejected as E_INPUT",p.program.name));},Err(_)=>campaign.refused+=1,}
   443	                }else{
   444	                    match engine.mutate(&p.program.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>campaign.refused+=1,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: matrix engine error {e}",p.program.name));},_=>{},}
   445	                }
   446	            }
   447	        }
   448	    }
   449	}
   450	
   451	fn fork(_bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
   452	    if !probes.is_empty(){return Err("fixture probes must be authored operations".into());}
   453	    fixture.fork_fixture().map_err(|e|e.to_string())
   454	}
   455	
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Stmt {
    Const { name: String, value: Expr },
    Let { name: String, value: Expr },
    Assign { name: String, value: Expr },
    Push { target: String, value: Expr },
    If { condition: Expr, then_body: Vec<Stmt>, else_body: Vec<Stmt> },
    For { binding: String, domain: LoopDomain, body: Vec<Stmt> },
    Require {
        condition: Expr,
        code: String,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        site: Option<String>,
        /// The author's text for the refusal (`require … else CODE 'message'`); the code stays the contract.
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        message: Option<String>,
    },
    /// A false predicate rolls the transaction back and re-reads `current`.
    Check { condition: Expr, current: Read },
    /// A unique clash yields absence, never the conflicting row.
    InsertIfAbsent { table: String, row: Expr, result: String },
    Insert { table: String, row: Expr },
    Update { table: String, key: Expr, patch: Expr },
    Upsert { table: String, key: Expr, row: Expr },
    /// Target lookup and the old image stay inside the engine. Only a read-filtered result is bound.
    WriteTarget { target: Read, patch: Expr, upsert: bool, condition: Option<WriteCondition>, result: Option<String> },
    Delete { table: String, key: Expr },
    /// Alternate-key delete resolves inside the engine before charging hidden expiry work.
    DeleteTarget { target: Read },
    Emit { effect: String, payload: Expr, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] once: Option<Expr> },
    Return(Expr),
    Break,
    Continue,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct WriteCondition {
    pub old: String,
    pub setup: Vec<Stmt>,
    pub value: Expr,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum LoopDomain {
    Page(Scan),
    LiteralRange { start: i64, end_exclusive: i64 },
    ExpressionRange { start: Expr, end_exclusive: Expr },
    ArgList { name: String, max: u32 },
    DerivedArray { value: Expr, max: u32, bound_source: String },
    Recurse(Recurse),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Recurse {
    pub site: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub index: String,
    pub seed: Box<Expr>,
    pub next_field: String,
    pub depth: u32,
    pub fanout: u32,
    pub provenance: KeyProvenance,
}

pub const MAX_RECURSE_DEPTH: u32 = 10_000;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Expr {
    Literal(Value),
    Variable(String),
    Input(Input),
    Field { base: Box<Expr>, name: String },
    Array(Vec<Expr>),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Expr>),
    Shape { base: Box<Expr>, fields: BTreeMap<String, Expr> },
    Binary { op: BinaryOp, left: Box<Expr>, right: Box<Expr> },
    Unary { op: UnaryOp, value: Box<Expr> },
    Builtin { builtin: Builtin, args: Vec<Expr> },
    Get(Read),
    Exists(Read),
    Block { body: Vec<Stmt>, value: Box<Expr> },
    Scan(Scan),
    Merge(Merge),
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Input {
    Viewer,
    Now,
    NewId,
    ReadTime,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum BinaryOp {
    Eq,
    Ne,
    }
}

/// Every table a program writes, by statement kind.
pub fn write_tables(program: &Program) -> Vec<(String, &'static str)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    stmts_write_tables(&program.body, &mut out);
    out
}

mod writes;
use writes::stmts_write_tables;

# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE means intent and the effective rule admit the same cases
in the bounded predicate kernel. Check the original requirement before rewriting; an equivalent policy
may be correct, but cannot detect a mistake copied into both declarations.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ means a role-wide branch gated by a row value exposes every column
on matching rows.
Use a board/projection table for a public rating; declare intent for the private
notes, principals, images and files separately.

`test` uses generation when fixtures are absent or contain no rows, including
an empty starter fixtures directory. Populated authored fixtures stay selected;
seeded read and integrity violations fail under that explicit starting-state
assumption. Generated payloads run authored creation mutations, preserving
requires, immutable copies and recipient-role checks; declined populations are
coverage gaps. A recorded role change or relationship transfer is shown in the
counterexample history; a stale starting snapshot is not such a history.

A completed `test` records concrete refutations under
`snapback/generated/security-test.json`. The next `check` reports matching
failures even when its short campaign cannot rediscover them. Exact source,
fixture bytes and CLI identity bind the record; edits invalidate it. No cached
passing result proves security. Every escalation note stays visible in short
output. Rule-changing deploy previews print exposure counts and demand a test
for the selected source/fixtures when a matching full finite campaign is absent.

//! SEC4: intent quality, typed generated populations, and bounded work.
use snapback4_lang::{compile, security::{self,challenges::{self,Campaign,Reachability}}};
fn bound(source:&str)->snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b
}
const ROLES:&str="use identity password\ntable roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('doctor','volunteer')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'\n  online only\n";
#[test]
fn equivalent_intent_is_advice_only_and_semantic_reordering_is_detected(){
    let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.read only viewer = .owner and allow\n";
    let b=bound(source);let r=security::analyze(&b);
    assert!(r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"&&n.site.line==7));
    assert!(!r.refuted());
    let b=bound(&source.replace("viewer = .owner and allow","viewer = .owner or viewer = 'service:operator'"));
    assert!(!security::analyze(&b).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"));
}
#[test]
fn per_role_population_catches_private_image_and_file_payloads_with_explicit_origin(){
    for field in ["scan: image <=5000", "scan: file <=5000 ('application/pdf')"] {
        let b=bound(&format!("{ROLES}table packages:\n  owner: principal\n  {field}\n  read <- exists roles[viewer]\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant packages.scan read only exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\nquery board(c: cursor ?):\n  return packages after c first 100 by .id\n"));
        let mut c=Campaign::default();c.generated_fixture=true;
        let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
        let roles=fixture.store().rows("roles").unwrap();
        for role in ["doctor","volunteer"] {
            assert_eq!(roles.iter().filter(|r|r.field("role").unwrap()==&snapback4_core::Value::String(role.into())).count(),2);
        }
        assert!(fixture.store().rows("packages").unwrap().len()>=2);
        challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
        let witnesses=c.violations.iter().filter(|v|v.reachability==Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        assert!(!witnesses.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
        assert!(witnesses.iter().any(|v|v.viewer.contains("volunteer")&&v.operation=="board"));
        let mut r=security::analyze(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&c);assert!(r.refuted());
        // A later synthetic witness must not replace the generated fixture provenance.
        let supplemental=challenges::run(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&supplemental);
        assert_eq!(r.invariants[0].counterexample.as_ref().unwrap().reachability,Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace);
    }
}
#[test]
fn generated_memberships_refer_to_valid_resource_and_distinct_owners(){
    let b=bound("table teams:\n  title: text 1..80\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ntable members:\n  team: teams\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('admin','member')\n  unique team, principal\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\n");
    let mut c=Campaign::default();let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
    let teams=fixture.store().rows("teams").unwrap();let members=fixture.store().rows("members").unwrap();
    assert!(teams.len()>=2&&members.len()>=2,"{c:?}");
    assert_ne!(members[0].field("principal"),members[1].field("principal"));
    assert!(members.iter().all(|m|teams.iter().any(|t|t.field("id")==m.field("team"))));
}

}

#[test]
fn rejected_fallback_final_exists_world_never_becomes_a_failing_fixture(){
    let source="table roots:\n  creator: principal\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  after insert <- exists members[root = .id]\n  online only\ntable members:\n  root: roots\n  proof: image <=1\n  unique root\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant roots.read only deny\nquery roots():\n  return roots first 100 by .id\n";
    let b=bound(source);let mut c=Campaign::default();
    assert!(challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).is_err(),"invalid final-exists seed must remain unsupported");
    challenges::generated_campaign(&b,&mut c,false);
    let digest = crate::release_files::digest(&root)?;
    if let Some(owner) = owner {
        let project = Project { root };
        let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
        crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
        crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
        let mut pointer = crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, previous, rollback)?;
        let activation = snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation {
            release,
            expected_previous: previous,
            expected_revision: Some(revision),
            restore_dormant,
            native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
            operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
        };
        let store = owner
            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared(
            store,
            bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
            &activation,
            allow_feed_adoption,
        )
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        pointer.finish(engine.generation())?;
        receipt(release,engine.generation(),&engine.store().adoption_report,options);

    } else {
        let result = crate::release_control::request_observed(
            dir,
            crate::release_control::Command::Activate {
                release,
                expected_release: previous,
                expected_revision: revision,
                digest,
                rollback,
                restore_dormant,
                allow_feed_adoption,
            },
            |pending| { if let Some(candidate)=candidate.as_deref_mut() {candidate.pending=pending;} },
        )?;
        if options.json {println!("{}",result);} else {
            println!("release {release} is current as generation {}; {}; running serve adopted the release",result["generation"],result["adoption"].as_str().unwrap_or("adopted"));
            for fill in result["backfills"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(|f|f.as_str()) {println!("  backfill: {fill}");}
            if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {println!("{}",result["details"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
    // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
    // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
    let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
    match result {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(error) => {
            eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
            1
        }
    }
}

pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    report(
        "deploy",

                else {serde_json::json!({"release":null,"revision":0,"generation":1})}
            } else {status(&dir, _deployment.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?};
            let previous = if let Some(owner) = _deployment.as_ref() {
                current(&dir, owner)?

                held["release"].as_u64()

            {

### 2.4 The client

**Generated facts.** For an ephemeral mutation, the generated operation
carries:
- `ephemeral: true`;
- `renews: true` when the binder marked it;
- `renewMs`: a quarter of the shortest life among the tables it writes, in
  milliseconds.

For every query, the generated operation carries `ephemeralReads`: the
static list of ephemeral tables its read closure can touch (read sites,
helpers, and native reads treated conservatively), omitted when empty.

The same facts appear on the operation entries of `/schema`.

```ts
export const setTyping: Ephemeral<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, null> =
  { kind: "mutation", name: "setTyping", runs: "server", predicts: "never", ephemeral: true, renews: true, renewMs: 1000 };
export const typingIn: Query<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, { principal: Principal }[]> =
  { kind: "query", name: "typingIn", runs: "server", ephemeralReads: ["typing"] };
```

**Types.** Durable and ephemeral mutations are disjoint types over a common
base, so widening cannot pick the wrong overload:

```ts
export type Mutation<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral?: false };
export type Ephemeral<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral: true;
  readonly renews?: true; readonly renewMs: number };
export type Signal<R = unknown> =
  | { readonly state: "sent"; readonly id: string; readonly result: R }
  | { readonly state: "failed"; readonly id: string; readonly why: Refusal };
```

`client.mutate(op, args)` resolves to `Signal<R>` for an `Ephemeral` and to
`Write<R>` for a `Mutation`. It takes no `MutateOptions` for an ephemeral
operation. `useMutation(op)` keeps its `{ run, last, inFlight }` shape and
types `run`'s result the same way. `usePendingWrite` accepts only
`Mutation`. Negative type tests pin all of this.

**Transport.** Calls travel the ordinary `POST /m/<name>` route, but they
bypass everything durable:

- the device outbox and its admission journal;
- the online tier's pending queue;
- `app-online.ts`'s durable journal;
- the browser admission path in `app-client.ts`;
- prediction.

**The ephemeral lane.** Each client has one ephemeral lane. It keeps at most
one request in flight and sends calls in call order. Keeping calls in order
means a `stopTyping` can never be overtaken by an earlier `setTyping`.

1. **Coalescing, `renews` operations only.**
   - The key is the operation name plus the canonical JSON of its arguments.
   - A new `renews` call whose key matches a call still waiting in the lane
     removes that waiting call and joins the end of the lane. The removed
     call's promise resolves with the new call's outcome.
   - A call that is already in flight is never replaced.
   - Non-`renews` operations are never coalesced.
2. **Renewal window, `renews` operations only.**
   - A call is not sent, and resolves at once with the previous result, if
     all of these hold:
     - its key equals that of the lane's last successfully sent call;
     - that call was sent less than `renewMs` ago;
     - the lane has sent no other call since, and none is waiting.
   - The rule is applied both when the call is made and when it reaches the
     head of the lane.
   - The skipped call's row is still alive for at least three quarters of
     its life.
   - A keystroke-per-call typing indicator therefore sends about once per
     `renewMs`, and its row expires between `0.75 × life` and `life` after
     the last keystroke.
3. **Offline.**
   - While the client's link is not `online`, a call resolves at once with
     `{ state: "failed", why: E_OFFLINE }`. It is never queued or retried.
   - When the link goes offline, every waiting call fails the same way.
   - `E_OFFLINE`'s card text gains that sentence: an ephemeral write is
     dropped, not held.
4. **Bounded.**
   - The lane holds at most 256 waiting calls.
   - Beyond that, a new call fails locally with `E_RATE_LIMIT` and is not
     sent.
5. **Refusals do not change the link.**
   - Engine refusals arrive as HTTP 200 with `{ state: "failed", why }`:
     rule, capacity, rate, generation. HTTP admission may instead return
     `{ denied }` with 503; the client validates and preserves that refusal
     without replaying the call or dropping waiting calls.
   - The lane changes the client's link state only on a transport failure:
     no response, or a response that is not this route's JSON.

**Watches need no new API.**
- A query with `ephemeralReads` joins the client's ephemeral interest set
  when it is observed, before its first answer.
- When that set grows, the held `/changes` request is aborted and re-issued
  at once. Shrinking never restarts it.
- A watch is re-run when a `/changes` answer names one of its ephemeral
  tables, and at its `nextExpiry`.
- In the local-first tier, this re-run never triggers a `/sync`, because
  ephemeral changes are not commits.
- §3.3 has the exact parameters and the fencing.

**Expiry recovery (2026-09-27, lane FK).** A local watch can receive a
server answer after its `nextExpiry`, including when the client's estimate
reaches that deadline just before the server does. Withhold that answer and
retry through the ordinary query/publication fences. Keep one request in
flight per watch; repeated expired replies back off at 25, 50, 100 ms and
so on, capped at 1 s, until a current answer arrives or the watch ends.
Without a new wakeup the first expired reply left the watch loading forever;
deadline expiry changes neither the durable sequence nor the ephemeral epoch.
`ephemeral-e2e.test.mjs` now forces the early estimate and keeps its original
empty-result and timing assertions; `expired-server-watch.test.mjs` covers
pacing, withholding, recovery and teardown, with and without `keep`.

The recipes the cards will teach:

```tsx
const typing = useQuery(api.typingIn, { roomId });
const setTyping = useMutation(api.setTyping);
const stopTyping = useMutation(api.stopTyping);
<Composer onChangeText={text => text ? setTyping.run({ roomId }) : stopTyping.run({ roomId })}
          onSend={() => stopTyping.run({ roomId })} />
<TypingIndicator visible={"data" in typing && (typing.data?.length ?? 0) > 0} />
```

The lane compiles this snippet against the real `Read<R>` union and adjusts
the narrowing to whatever the union requires. The card shows the compiled
form.

For presence in a scope ("who has this chat open"), declare
`table viewing … ephemeral 30s` with a `renews` `markViewing` operation. The
card's snippet calls it on mount and every 10 s, and calls a delete
operation on unmount.
