fn engine_on_store(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probes:Vec<Program>,store:Store)->Result<Engine,String>{
    let mut programs=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();programs.extend(probes);
    let mut engine=Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),programs,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let mut seeded=rows.iter().filter(|(name,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).map(|(name,rs)|{
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
        let clean=rs.iter().map(|r|if let Value::Row(fields)=r {Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect())}else{r.clone()}).collect();
        (name.clone(),clean)
    }).collect::<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>();
    for (table,values) in &mut seeded {for row in values {for (column,kind) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns {
        if let Ok(v)=row.field(column) {
            let value=hydrate(&mut engine,kind,v)?;
            if let Value::Row(fields)=row {fields.insert(column.clone(),value);}
        }
    }}}
    engine.seed_at(seeded.iter().flat_map(|(t,rs)|rs.iter().map(move|r|(t.clone(),r.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>(),NOW).map_err(|e|format!("generated snapshot rejected by schema: {e}"))?;
    validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;
    Ok(engine)
}
fn validate_fixture(bound:&Bound,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<(),String>{
    let actual=readback(engine)?;
    for (name,values) in &actual {for row in values {
        snapback4_core::engine::validate::validate_row(&bound.schema,name,row).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        for c in &bound.schema.tables[name].constraints {if let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{table,index,key}=c {
            let rule=RuleExpr::Exists{table:table.clone(),index:index.clone(),key:key.clone(),provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed,equalities:Vec::new()};
            if engine.fixture_policy(&rule,snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,None,row,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=rules::Verdict::Readable {return Err(format!("{name}: trusted seed did not establish final-exists constraint"));}
        }}
    }}
    Ok(())
}

fn replay(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,word:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(bool,Value),String>{
    let name="$security.probe";let table=inv.table.clone();let key=id(&table);
    let read=Read{site:"security challenge".into(),table:table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(key.clone())],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed};
    let mut seeds=rows.clone();
    let probe=match word{
        "read"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(read))]),
        "insert"=>{seeds.remove(&table);program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Insert{table,row:Expr::Literal(row.clone())},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])},
        "delete"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Delete{table,key:Expr::Literal(key)},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]),
        "update"=>{
            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
            let changed=definition.columns.iter().filter(|(n,_)|n.as_str()!="id"&&!definition.engine_field(n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|inv.column.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|c==*n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|!definition.constraints.iter().any(|c|matches!(c,Constraint::Immutable{fields} if fields.contains(n))))
                .find_map(|(n,k)| {let old=row.field(n).ok()?;let next=match (k.required(),old){
                    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
                    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|v|Some(Value::String((*v).clone()))!=Some(old.clone()))?.clone()),
                    (C::Text{max},Value::String(v)) if v.chars().count()<*max as usize=>Value::String(format!("{v}x")),
                    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(v))=>Value::Int(v+1),_=>return None};
                    Some((n.clone(),next))});
            let Some((column,value))=changed else{return Err("no valid changed mutable-column sample".into());};
            program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Update{table,key:Expr::Literal(key),patch:Expr::Literal(Value::Map([(column,value)].into_iter().collect()))},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])
        },_=>return Err("row integrity/transition challenge is not an operation admission test".into()),
    };
    let mut engine=engine(bound,&seeds,probe)?;
    if word=="read"{return engine.query(name,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map(|q|(q.data!=Value::Null,row.clone())).map_err(|e|e.to_string());}
    let outcome=engine.mutate(name,None,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    if !matches!(outcome.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}){return Ok((false,row.clone()));}
    let next=engine.store().rows(&inv.table).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.into_iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
    if word=="delete"{return if next.is_none(){Ok((true,row.clone()))}else{Err("delete returned sent without removing the target".into())};}
    let next=next.ok_or("sent write has no committed target image")?;
    if word=="update" {
        let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
        let changed=table.columns.keys().filter(|c|!table.engine_field(c)&&!table.maintained.contains_key(*c))
            .any(|c|row.field(c).ok()!=next.field(c).ok()&&inv.column.as_ref().is_none_or(|column|column==c));
        if !changed{return Err("sent no-op/implicit-only update has no changed declared target column".into());}
    }
    Ok((true,next))
}
fn readback(engine:&Engine)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{engine.schema().tables.keys().map(|t|engine.store().rows(t).map(|rs|(t.clone(),rs)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).collect()}

fn operation_preflight(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->Result<(),String>{
    let Some(name)=&inv.operation else{return Ok(());};
    let p=&bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==name).ok_or("operation did not bind")?.program;
    if !super::census::body(&p.body){return Err(format!("{name}: opaque/loop/scan path requires write-boundary tracing"));}
    let rule=inv.policy.as_ref().ok_or("operation policy outside rule fragment")?;
    if rule.any_term(&|t|!matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))){return Err("operation policy requires write-boundary row-image tracing".into());}
            }
            let candidate_args=inv.operation.as_ref().and_then(|op|bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op)).and_then(|p|arguments(&p.program.args,variant));
            // Pruned dependencies can repeat after unrelated schema enum digits.
            // Identical seed/typed-argument candidates are one search input.
            if !seen.insert(serde_json::to_string(&(&rows,&candidate_args)).unwrap()){continue;}
            let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(Read{site:"security read".into(),table:inv.table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(id(&inv.table))],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed}))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let rows=match readback(&seeded){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let Some(row)=rows.get(&inv.table).and_then(|r|r.first())else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no generated ordinary row",inv.table));break;};
            for viewer in [VIEWER,OTHER,"account:security-no-role","service:security"]{
                if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                if inv.target=="always" {
                    match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){
                        Ok(false)=>{out.attempted+=1;out.admitted+=1;out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::TrustedSeedOnly,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:"$security.seed_at".into(),effect:"always".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:rows.clone(),rule_path:"trusted seed ingress; all typed and final-exists constraints rechecked".into(),scope:"actual seed_at commit/read-back; ordinary application reachability is not claimed".into()});found=true;},
                        Ok(true)=>{out.attempted+=1;},Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);}
                    }if found{break;}continue;
                }
                if let Some(operation)=&inv.operation {
                    let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==operation)else{continue;};
                    let permitted=match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){Ok(p)=>p,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: operation intent not judged: {why}"));continue;}};
                    let Some(args)=arguments(&p.program.args,variant)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: arguments need fixture assets or unsupported samples"));continue;};
                    if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut seeded,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}"));continue;}};
                    match seeded.mutate(operation,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)) {
                        Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..})=>{
                            let current=readback(&seeded).unwrap_or_default();
                            let changed=current.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|!rows.get(&inv.table).is_some_and(|old|old.contains(r))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                            for next in changed {
                                let old=rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|old|old.field("id").ok()==next.field("id").ok());
                                // An upsert must be attributed to its executed arm;
                                // a column contract cannot be refuted by editing a sibling.
                                if inv.target=="update"&&old.is_none() || inv.target=="insert"&&old.is_some() || inv.target=="delete" {continue;}
                                if let Some(column)=&inv.column && old.is_some_and(|old|old.field(column).ok()==next.field(column).ok()){continue;}
                                if !permitted{out.admitted+=1;out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::SyntheticOperation,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:args.clone(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:operation.clone(),effect:if old.is_some(){"update"}else{"insert"}.into(),row:next.clone(),old:old.cloned(),supporting_rows:rows.clone(),rule_path:format!("authored operation {operation}"),scope:"actual authored mutation committed a changed image from a schema-valid seeded snapshot".into()});found=true;break;}
                            }
                        },_=>out.refused+=1,
                    }
                    if found{break;}continue;
                }
                for word in super::effects(inv){
                    let mut admission_rows=rows.clone();if word=="insert"{admission_rows.remove(&inv.table);}
                    let judged=if word=="read"{policy_in(bound,inv,&admission_rows,viewer,Some(row),row,&mut seeded)}else{policy(bound,inv,&admission_rows,viewer,(word!="insert").then_some(row),row)};
                    let allowed=match judged{Ok(v)=>v,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                    if allowed{continue;}
                    if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    let replayed=if word=="read" {seeded.query("$security.seed",Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map(|q|(q.data!=Value::Null,row.clone())).map_err(|e|e.to_string())}else{replay(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,word,row)};
                    match replayed{
                        Ok((false,_))=>out.refused+=1,
                        Ok((true,next))=>{
                            let mut args=BTreeMap::new();
                            if word=="update"{let changes=bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.keys().filter(|c|row.field(c).ok()!=next.field(c).ok()).filter_map(|c|next.field(c).ok().map(|v|(c.clone(),v.clone()))).collect();args.insert("$syntheticPatch".into(),Value::Map(changes));}
                            out.admitted+=1;out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::SyntheticOperation,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args,viewer:viewer.into(),operation:format!("$security.{}.{}",inv.table,word),effect:word.into(),row:next,old:(word=="update"||word=="delete").then(||row.clone()),supporting_rows:admission_rows.clone(),rule_path:bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].rules.for_effect(super::effect(word)).quarry(),scope:if word=="update"{"schema-valid seeded snapshot; old-image policy false at an actual changed update; committed next image and patch shown; application reachability is not claimed".into()}else{"schema-valid seeded snapshot; synthetic engine table operation; application reachability is not claimed".into()}});found=true;break;},
                        Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: {e}",inv.table,word));},
                    }
                }
                if found{break;}
            }
            if found{break;}
        }
    }
    column_reads::generated(bound,&mut out,sites);
    paired::run(bound,&mut out,sites);
    timed::run(bound,&mut out,sites);
    out.unsupported.insert("unauthenticated HTTP attempts require a live server; engine principals are authenticated contexts".into());
    out.unsupported.insert("offline/revoked-device access and retained history require device boundary qualification".into());out
}
pub fn apply(report:&mut Report,campaign:&Campaign){
    report.campaign_coverage.extend(campaign.unsupported.iter().cloned());
    let rank=|c:&Counterexample|match c.reachability{Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace=>0,Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace=>1,_=>2};
    let committed=|c:&Counterexample|c.history.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
    for c in &campaign.violations {for v in &mut report.invariants {if v.invariant.site.to_string()!=c.invariant_site{continue;}
        if v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|old|rank(old)<rank(c)||rank(old)==rank(c)&&committed(old)<=committed(c)){continue;}
        v.status=Status::Refuted;v.counterexample=Some(c.clone());
        v.reasons.push("concrete policy-false snapshot admitted by the actual engine; fixture origin is explicit, not an empty-state reachability theorem".into());
    }}
}

fn arguments(args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,variant:usize)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Value>>{
    use ir::ArgType as A;
fn dominating_argument_guard_suppresses_excluded_role_and_names_actual_risk(){
    let source=format!("{ROLES}table docs:\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation register(role: enum('doctor','volunteer')):\n  require role = 'volunteer' else NOT_ALLOWED\n  return insert roles {{principal: viewer,role}}\n").replace("insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'","insert <- .principal = viewer");
    let b=bound(&source);let mut r=security::analyze(&b);security::add_source_notes(&mut r,&[("schema.q".into(),source.clone())],&b);
    assert!(!r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION"),"{:?}",r.notes);
    let source=source.replace("  require role = 'volunteer' else NOT_ALLOWED\n","");
    let b=bound(&source);let mut r=security::analyze(&b);security::add_source_notes(&mut r,&[("schema.q".into(),source)],&b);
    assert!(r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION"&&n.message.contains("'doctor'")),"{:?}",r.notes);
}

## 3. Semantics

### 3.1 Time

- Deadlines use the server clock (`Shared::now`). That is the development
  clock under `dev`, so `dev clock advance` expires ephemeral rows as it
  expires `expire at` rows.
- A row is absent at `now >= deadline`, through the same `expired` check the
  runner already applies to expiring tables. The engine gives ephemeral
  tables a hidden `$deadline` column and an `$expiry` index, like
  `through`'s hidden deadline (`schema/through.rs`).
- **`$deadline` never reaches the wire.**
  - `recorded.rs` `public_image` returns a row unchanged when the table has
    no recorded fields, no `through` and no timeline slots. Ordinary
    ephemeral tables have exactly that shape. That fast path must also
    check for engine fields.
  - `value/disclosed.rs` `Disclosed::visible` must hide `$deadline` as it
    hides recorded fields.
  - Wire tests cover unshaped point reads, scans, and rows returned by
    mutations.
- Generated row types omit engine fields (`emit.rs:24`).

### 3.2 Authorization and revocation

- Ephemeral rows are read under their table's read rule, like any row.
- Rules are evaluated at read time, and ephemeral rows are never cached on a
  device. So a principal who loses membership sees nothing on their next
  read of that room.
- A durable commit to a table a watched query probes invalidates that
  query, as today.
- By the renewal law (§2.2), a departed member's rows outlive the departure
  by at most one life, whatever the update rule says.
- **Disclosure.** The `/changes` answer names ephemeral tables that were
  written. That is the same class as today's table-granular durable
  invalidation, which names every table a commit touched (`serve_changes.rs:80-94`).
  It adds no new leak class. Capacity refusals depend only on the writer's
  own rows (§3.5), except for the global backstop, which reveals only a
  server-wide aggregate, like HTTP admission.

### 3.3 Change notification

**Server state.**
- The memory store keeps:
  - an **epoch**, a `u64` that each committed ephemeral mutation
    increments;
  - an **incarnation**, a random id that changes whenever the store is
    cleared (§3.4);
  - a bounded ring of `(epoch, tables written)`.
- Deadline expiry does not bump the epoch.
  - For a **complete** answer this is safe. No rule probes an ephemeral table
    (§2.1), so expiry can change the answer only by removing rows the answer
    read. Those rows' deadlines are in its `nextExpiry`, and clients
    re-fetch at it.
  - An **incomplete** answer (`complete: false`, for example a scan cut by
    the authorization walk allowance behind hidden rows) can become more
    complete when hidden rows expire, and their deadlines cannot be
    exported. So a watched query with `ephemeralReads` whose latest answer
    is incomplete is re-fetched on a fixed, content-independent 1 s cadence
    while it stays incomplete. (Revision 3: Astra's delta probe put 8,192
    hidden rows ahead of a live row.)
  - The sweep only reclaims memory.

**Wake state, and no lost wakeups.**
- Polls get their own wake state: `Shared.wake: Mutex<Wake { seq, store_identity, generation, eph_incarnation, eph_epoch, ring_tail }>`,
  with a `polls: Condvar`.
- A durable commit updates `seq` under that mutex and notifies `polls`. It
  also notifies `committed` as today, for maintenance.
- An ephemeral commit publishes `(incarnation, epoch, ring tail)` into the
  wake state **while still holding the hub**, taking the wake mutex last
  (hub, then wake), and notifies `polls` only. `clear_and_rebind` publishes
  its new incarnation and hub binding (store identity, generation) together
  under that mutex. Wake publication therefore follows
  commit order. (Revision 3: publishing after releasing the hub let a paused
  commit 1 overwrite commit 2's wake. Astra's protocol model reproduced it.)
- A poll reads its wake snapshot under the wake mutex before `wait_timeout`,
  and re-reads it under the same mutex after a wake.
- On a wake where `seq` is unchanged, a poll takes no engine lock. Only a
  moved `seq` takes the engine for the store check, as today.
- Lock order is: engine mutex, then hub, then wake mutex. Nothing takes the
  hub or the engine while holding the wake mutex. Nothing takes the engine
  while holding the hub.

**`GET /changes` parameters.**
- `eph=<epoch>`.
- `ephi=<incarnation>`.
- `ephTables=<comma-separated table names>`:
  - absent means "wake me for every ephemeral commit";
  - present, possibly empty, means "wake me only for these tables".
- Clients send all three when the backend declares an ephemeral table.
  A client with no ephemeral interest sends `ephTables=` (empty). A
  durable-only backend keeps the old wire: clients omit all three, with no
  initial incarnation reset (§3.6).
- An online client without schema facts learns support from
  `X-Snapback-Ephemeral: 1` on ordinary responses/heartbeats, present only for
  ephemeral backends and exposed through CORS. It loads `/schema` and reissues
  any legacy discovery poll immediately. Generated read facts can enable the
  parameters before discovery. This preserves the entire durable-only request
  flow, including clients whose transport has no `/schema` endpoint.

**When the poll answers.** Exactly as today, plus two conditions:
- the store's sequence moved past `since`;
- the store identity changed;
- the generation differs from the generation at poll entry;
- the wait expired;
- a table in its interest was written after `eph` (new);
- the incarnation differs from `ephi` (new).

**Wake filtering and pacing.**
- A wake caused only by an ephemeral write outside the interest keeps
  waiting. It neither answers nor restarts the heartbeat.
- If the ring no longer covers `eph`, the answer names every table in the
  interest.
- An answer caused only by ephemeral writes is not sent sooner than 100 ms
  after the poll began. That bounds each client to 10 ephemeral-only
  re-polls a second, however fast a session writes.

**What the answer carries.**
- It gains `"ephemeral": { "epoch": n, "incarnation": "…", "tables": [...] }`.
- A poll with none of the three parameters answers exactly as before (the
  field omitted), apart from waking for ephemeral commits. A schema with no
  ephemeral table has none, so for it this is byte-identical to today.

**Re-issuing a poll.**
- Backend adoption reconciles every mounted watch against the adopted
  operation facts, including additions and removals. The client re-issues its
  held poll whenever its ephemeral interest set grows (§2.4).
- The re-issued poll's `eph` is the smallest epoch among the current
  incarnation's latest answers of its ephemeral watches.
- A watch with no answer yet contributes the client's last `/changes`
  epoch.
- A write that lands between an answer and the new poll is therefore always
  after `eph`, and the new poll answers at once.

**Query answers.**
- A query answer that read an ephemeral table carries
  `"ephemeral": { "epoch": n, "incarnation": "…" }`.
- The epoch is read under the hub lock, in the same critical section as the
  memory rows.

**Fencing.**
- The client keeps an **ephemeral revision**, a counter it tags onto every
  ephemeral query request and `/changes` poll it sends.
- Accepting a new incarnation advances the revision. The client then
  invalidates its outstanding ephemeral queries and its poll, and re-issues
  them.
- A response tagged with an older revision is dropped before its
  incarnation is looked at. So `A:50 → B:1 → delayed A:50` cannot reinstate
  the retired incarnation A. (Revision 3: under revision 2's rule, a
  different incarnation was always accepted. Astra's protocol model
  reproduced the resurrection.)
- Within the current revision, each watch remembers its own
  `(incarnation, epoch)` floor.
- An answer from a different incarnation is accepted, advances the
  revision, and replaces the floor.
- An answer from the same incarnation is accepted only if its epoch is at
  least the floor.
- This is independent of durable `seq` fencing (`minimumReadSeq`), and the
  two are never folded together.
- It applies in the online tier's `currentRead`, in `local-query.ts`'s
  publication path, and to `/changes` answers before they are judged stale.

**Presence re-polls.** Poll entry today runs `presence_reconnected`, which
opens an IMMEDIATE transaction (`engine/effects.rs:287-291`). With this
change, it first checks without a write transaction whether the principal
has a pending presence deferral. It opens the write transaction only if it
has one. The effect semantics are unchanged; ephemeral re-polls stop paying
a writer transaction each.

### 3.4 Generation fence, restart, adoption

The memory store is bound to the `(store identity, generation)` it serves.

- Every hub access runs under the hub lock, for mutations and for queries.
  It compares the durable snapshot's `(store identity, generation)`, which
  its read transaction has already established, with the hub's binding:
  - **hub older:** clear it, rebind to the snapshot's pair, and change the
    incarnation;
  - **snapshot older:** refuse with the store's retryable generation
    refusal. The request retries on a fresh reader.
- Adoption (`serve_adoption.rs`) holds the engine mutex. In-place activation
  checks the hub for poison, releases it throughout the SQLite transaction,
  then takes it to clear and rebind after commit. It never holds the hub while
  waiting for SQLite readers: those readers take SQLite before the hub, and a
  DELETE-mode writer needs them to finish before commit or spill. A hub poisoned
  after the initial check is completely reset at committed rebind, avoiding a
  post-commit refusal (LLP 3000.033 §1.3). Engine replacement (`open_engine_at`)
  likewise clears and rebinds the shared hub before publishing the new engine.
- A reader loaned before adoption can therefore never write under the old
  generation's indexes into the new store, nor read new rows under old
  rules.

Ephemeral rows do not survive any of these:
- a server restart;
- schema adoption under `dev`;
- a generation change;
- a store replacement.

Each changes the incarnation, so every client watching an ephemeral table
re-runs its queries (§3.3 fencing).

Day-two classification (`successor.rs`):
- **Destructive:** a durable table gaining `ephemeral`. Activation deletes
  }
 }
}

#[test]
fn immutable_column_pairs_use_independent_valid_worlds(){
 let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  immutable owner, body\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.body read only .owner = viewer\nquery inspect(note: notes):\n  n = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return n.body\n";
 let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
 let now=1_800_000_000_000;let mut fixture=Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),now).unwrap();
 fixture.seed_at([("notes".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("n".into())),("owner",Value::Id("dev:fixture-owner".into())),("body",Value::String("secret".into()))]))],now).unwrap();
 let before=fixture.store().rows("notes").unwrap();let mut c=security::challenges::Campaign::default();security::challenges::operations(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
 assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.effect=="column read observation"&&v.reachability==security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace),"{c:?}");assert_eq!(fixture.store().rows("notes").unwrap(),before);
}
#[test]
fn hidden_reference_hops_stay_unknown_and_expiry_is_not_an_authored_delete(){
 let source="table parents:\n  owner: principal\n  active: bool\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\ntable notes:\n  parent: parents\n  until: time\n  read <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  expire at .until\n  online only\ninvariant notes.read only .parent.active != true\ninvariant notes.delete only deny\nquery inspect(note: notes):\n  return notes[note]\nmutation expire(note: notes):\n  update notes[note] { until: now }\n  return null\n";
fn projection_only_fixture_observers_do_not_disclose_column_values(){
 let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.body read only .owner = viewer\nquery inspect(note: notes):\n  n = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return { id: n.id }\n";
 let c=fixture_campaign(source,vec![("notes".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("n".into())),("owner",Value::Id("dev:owner".into())),("body",Value::String("secret".into()))]))]);assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
}
#[test]
fn column_mutation_cannot_change_transitive_reference_authority(){
 let source="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  active: bool\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\ntable parents:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, active = true]\n  online only\ntable notes:\n  parent: parents\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.body read only .parent.owner = viewer\nmutation revoke(note: notes):\n  update roles[viewer] { active: false }\n  n = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return n.body\n";
 let c=fixture_campaign(source,vec![("roles".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("r".into())),("principal",Value::Id("dev:reader".into())),("active",Value::Bool(true))])),("parents".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("p".into())),("owner",Value::Id("dev:owner".into()))])),("notes".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("n".into())),("parent",Value::Id("p".into())),("body",Value::String("secret".into()))]))]);assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");assert!(c.unsupported.iter().any(|s|s.contains("before disclosure")),"{c:?}");
}

fn unchanged_authored_fixture_assertion_failures_are_reachable(){

}

 let mut source="table notes:\n  n: int\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n".to_string();
 for n in 0..600{source.push_str(&format!("query q{n}(mode: enum('a','b','c','d','e','f','g','h')):\n  return notes first 1 by .id\n"));}
}

#[test]
fn authored_query_refusal_is_a_column_value_observation() {
 let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text 1..40\n  read <- allow\n  online only\nquery observe(note: notes):\n  n = notes[note] ! else MISSING\n  require n.body = 'secret' else DIFFERENT 'protected value differs'\n  return 0\ninvariant notes.body read only .owner = viewer\n";
 let c=fixture_campaign(source,vec![("notes".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("n".into())),("owner",Value::Id("dev:owner".into())),("body",Value::String("secret".into()))]))]);
 let v=c.violations.iter().find(|v|v.effect=="column read observation"&&v.reachability==security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace).unwrap_or_else(||panic!("{c:?}"));
 let observations=serde_json::to_string(&v.observations).unwrap();
 assert!(observations.contains("DIFFERENT")&&observations.contains("protected value differs"),"{observations}");
}

#[test]
fn demotion_is_followed_by_writes_as_the_affected_principal() {
 let source="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('inspector','landowner')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  update <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  online only\ntable reports:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nmutation changeRole(who: principal, role: enum('inspector','landowner')):\n  update roles[who] { role }\n  return null\nmutation record():\n  return insert reports { owner: viewer }\ninvariant reports.insert only exists roles[viewer, role = 'inspector']\n";
 let c=fixture_campaign(source,vec![("roles".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("r".into())),("principal",Value::Id("dev:inspector".into())),("role",Value::String("inspector".into()))]))]);
 assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.viewer=="dev:inspector"&&v.effect=="insert"&&v.history.len()==2&&v.history[0].operation=="changeRole"&&v.history[1].operation=="record"),"{c:?}");
}

#[test]
fn pending_rows_keep_their_write_arm_and_executed_delete() {
# Ephemeral tables: typing and presence in a room

Ephemeral tables hold short-lived rows in one server process, under ordinary table rules. Use them for typing and viewing indicators that disappear without durable writes.

```quarry
table rooms:
  name: text <=80
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
  sync to members[.id]

table members:
  roomId: rooms
  principal: principal
  unique roomId, principal
  by byPrincipal: principal, roomId
  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  sync to members[.roomId] last 100 by .id

table typing:
  roomId: rooms
  principal: principal
  unique roomId, principal
  cap 50 by roomId
  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  update <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  delete <- .principal = viewer
  ephemeral 4s

mutation setTyping(roomId: rooms):
  upsert typing[roomId, viewer] {}

mutation stopTyping(roomId: rooms):
  delete typing[roomId, viewer]

query typingIn(roomId: rooms):
  return typing where .roomId = roomId and .principal != viewer by .principal { principal }

query typingInMyRooms(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 100 by .roomId after c { roomId, typing: typing[roomId = .roomId] where .principal != viewer { principal } }

table viewing:
  roomId: rooms
  principal: principal
  unique roomId, principal
  cap 50 by roomId
  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  update <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  delete <- .principal = viewer
  ephemeral 30s

mutation markViewing(roomId: rooms):
  upsert viewing[roomId, viewer] {}

mutation stopViewing(roomId: rooms):
  delete viewing[roomId, viewer]

query viewingIn(roomId: rooms):
  return viewing where .roomId = roomId by .principal { principal }
```

Keep the ordinary chats-list query device-held. Observe `typingInMyRooms`
separately with `{ keep: true }` and render its room hints beside that list: never join an
ephemeral table into a query that must work offline.

The ephemeral case must be decided before the generic "removed; its rows
are lost" branch (`successor.rs:65-69`). The `$deadline` engine-field
branch is not the only step.

### 3.5 Bounds

| Bound | Value | Refusal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Life | 1 s – 60 s | `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL` at `check`; generation admission re-checks |
| Live rows written by one principal | 1,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes written by one principal | 4 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Live rows, all principals (backstop) | 100,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes, all principals (backstop) | 512 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Ephemeral mutations per session | 20 per second sustained, burst 40 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` |
| Waiting calls in a client's lane | 256 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` (local) |
| Rows per group | the table's `cap … by …` | the existing cap refusal |
| Work per mutation and per query | the existing meter | unchanged |

Every refusal in this table is retryable, carries no row detail, and
arrives as HTTP 200 `{ state: "failed", why }` (§2.4 rule 5).

**How capacity is counted.**
- The hub records each row's writing principal.
- A row is charged its **declared maximum image size**, computed from the
  schema's bounds for its columns and index keys, never its actual bytes.
- Per-principal admission therefore depends only on the writer's own rows
  and on the schema. Another principal's private payload cannot flip it
  (Astra F3).
- The global backstop reveals only a server-wide aggregate. That is the
  same residual class as HTTP admission being full.

**When capacity is checked.**
- Capacity counts live rows only. The hub sweeps expired rows before it
  refuses.
- The check runs under the hub lock, after the program and before the epoch
  bump. A refusal undoes the journal: the row is absent and the epoch does
  not move.

**The rate bucket is new.**
- It is a new per-session token bucket, checked before the mutation runs.
- It applies under `dev` and `serve` alike.
- It shares nothing with the login and upload counters that `E_RATE_LIMIT`
  names today. The code is reused, its card text is extended, and its
  family stays `auth`.

The lane's coalescing and renewal window keep an ordinary typing indicator
near one request per second per typist. That is far below these bounds.

### 3.6 What does not change, and what the lock claims are

**What does not change.**
- A schema with no ephemeral table serializes byte-identically. Its wire,
  its device and the published-0.2.33 capability fences are unchanged.
- A new capability name, `ephemeral`, makes an older device refuse a schema
  that has one, instead of queuing its writes.
- `connected[p]` keeps its meaning and its grace.
- Durable mutations, their prediction, the outbox, replay and the partition
  log are unchanged.
- Presence-guarded effects are unchanged.

**The lock claims.** An ephemeral mutation:
- never opens a SQLite write transaction;
- never holds the serving engine's mutex while it executes;
- takes that mutex only for the short sections a query takes: session
  resolution and reader checkout.

Only queries and mutations whose static read closure includes an ephemeral
table take the hub lock, and only for their metered run. Message pages and
other durable queries never wait on it. `MemoryRows` is `!Sync`, so this is
a `Mutex`, not an `RwLock`; the programs that take it are small.

## 4. Implementation

The work runs in two codex `gpt-6-astra` xhigh lanes on the Apartment M5 Pro
mini, from `origin/main` at launch:

- **E1** — compiler, core, engine, server, device guard, guide cards;
  branch `sb4/eph-engine`.
- **E2** — the TypeScript client; branch `sb4/eph-client`, started from
  E1's server checkpoint so that its tests run the real CLI.

File references are from main `71f77c16ca`. The lanes re-verify each
citation.

### 4.1 Language (`crates/snapback4-lang`)

- `ast.rs` `TableDecl` (327-344): `ephemeral: Option<EphemeralDecl { life_ms, site }>`.
- `parser.rs` table body (188-313): add an `ephemeral` arm beside `expire`
  (236-251) and `online` (252-260), reusing `parser/views.rs` `duration_unit`.
- `bind/schema.rs`:
  - `bind_ephemeral` beside `bind_expire` (236-245, 270-283);
  - the refusals of §2.1 in `bind_table` (133-265), `probe` (514) for
    every table, `bind_sync` (285+) and reference columns;
  - the hidden `$deadline` column and `$expiry` index, mirroring
    `bind/through.rs` (218-248).
- A new exhaustive effect visitor (for example `bind/effects.rs`, shared
  with `engine/read_only.rs` if that fits):
  - it classifies programs and computes `renews` and `ephemeralReads`;
  - it drives §2.2's refusals from `bind/mod.rs` (122-140);
  - it sets `ephemeral`, `renews`, `renewMs` and `predicts = "never"` on
    mutation facts, and `ephemeralReads` on query facts;
  - it also produces the `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER` note.
- `bind/residency.rs` (`site` 181-202, `table_line` 494-503,
  `operation_lines` 505-535): the ephemeral residency line.
- `emit.rs` (61-66, 98-104; the import at 19): the facts of §2.4, and the
  `Ephemeral` import when used.
- `guide.rs` `KEYWORDS` (57): the `ephemeral` card and the `typing` alias.
- `guide/codes.rs`:
  - add `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` and `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`;
  - extend `E_RATE_LIMIT`'s text to ephemeral writes;
  - amend `E_OFFLINE` (193).
- `guide/reads.rs` (95-130), `guide/presence.rs` and `tests/guide.rs`:
  rewrite `w10b_typing_card_compiles_and_expires_at_the_deadline`
  (229-244) for the ephemeral recipe, and keep a durable `expire at` test.

### 4.2 Core schema (`crates/snapback4-core`)

}
fn role_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut groups=Vec::new();
    let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
    if !ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(table,_)|!super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table).is_empty()){return out;}
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return Vec::new();}
    let Ok(mut judge)=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())else{return Vec::new();};
    for (table,_) in ir::write_tables(p){
        let columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,&table);
        if !columns.iter().any(|c|json.contains(&format!("\"{c}\":"))){continue;}
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
        for row in initial.get(&table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let principal=t.columns.iter().find_map(|(c,k)|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal).then(||row.field(c).ok()).flatten());
            let Some(principal)=principal else{continue;};
            let mut targets=Vec::new();
            for case in cases{
                let mut args=case.clone();
                for (name,kind) in &p.args{match kind{
                    ir::ArgType::Principal=>{args.insert(name.clone(),principal.clone());},
                    ir::ArgType::Ref{table:target} if target==&table=>{if let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(name.clone(),id.clone());}},_=>{}
                }}
                if columns.iter().any(|c|args.get(c).is_some_and(|v|row.field(c).ok()==Some(v))){continue;}
                if !targets.contains(&args){targets.push(args);}
            }
            let mut row_cases=Vec::new();
            for actor in actors{
                let owner=columns.iter().filter_map(|c|t.written_by.get(c)).any(|a|row.field(&a.owner).ok()==Some(&Value::Id(actor.clone())));
                if !campaign.reserve(1){return out;}
                let gate=judge.fixture_policy(&t.update_rule_judged(),actor,Some(row),row,NOW).ok();
                if !owner&&gate!=Some(rules::Verdict::Readable){continue;}
                for args in &targets{row_cases.push((p,actor.clone(),args.clone()));}
            }
            groups.push(row_cases);
        }
    }
    for n in 0..groups.iter().map(Vec::len).max().unwrap_or(0){for g in &groups{if let Some(case)=g.get(n){out.push(case.clone());}}}
    out
}

//! Independent intent must catch a correctly-enforced but incorrect rule.
use snapback4_lang::{compile,security::{self,Status}};
fn bound(source:&str)->snapback4_lang::Bound{let b=compile(&[("policy.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b}
fn basic(rule:&str)->String{format!("table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text 1..80\n  read <- {rule}\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  update <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\ninvariant notes.read only .owner = viewer\n")}
#[test]fn independent_policy_refutes_broad_read_and_fixed_rule_passes(){
    let b=bound(&basic("allow"));let mut r=security::analyze(&b);assert_eq!(r.invariants[0].status,Status::Unproved);
    let c=security::challenges::run(&b);assert!(!c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");security::challenges::apply(&mut r,&c);assert_eq!(r.invariants[0].status,Status::Refuted);
    let b=bound(&basic(".owner = viewer"));assert_eq!(security::analyze(&b).invariants[0].direct_rule,Status::Proved);assert!(security::challenges::run(&b).violations.is_empty());
}
#[test]fn retained_assignment_does_not_prove_current_role(){
    let schema="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('inspector', 'owner')\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  update <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  online only\ntable grants:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  online only\ntable reports:\n  body: text 1..80\n  read <- exists grants[viewer]\n  online only\ninvariant reports.read only exists roles[viewer, role = 'inspector']\n";
    let b=bound(schema);let c=security::challenges::run(&b);assert!(!c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
    let b=bound(&schema.replace("read <- exists grants[viewer]","read <- exists grants[viewer] and exists roles[viewer, role = 'inspector']"));assert_eq!(security::analyze(&b).invariants[0].direct_rule,Status::Proved);assert!(security::challenges::run(&b).violations.is_empty());
}

#[test]
fn column_read_uses_read_authority_and_authored_projection_observers(){
    let src="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text 1..80\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.body read only .owner = viewer\nquery inspect(note: notes):\n  n = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return n.body\n";
    let b=bound(src);assert_eq!(b.invariants[0].column.as_deref(),Some("body"));
    assert_ne!(security::analyze(&b).invariants[0].direct_rule,Status::Proved,"denied delete cannot prove a column read");
    let c=security::challenges::run(&b);assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.effect=="column read observation"),"{c:?}");
    let safe=bound(&src.replace("return n.body","return { owner: n.owner }"));
    assert!(security::challenges::run(&safe).violations.is_empty(),"projection excluding protected value is not a whole-row disclosure");
    let audit=security::analyze(&b);assert!(audit.exposures[0].column_audiences.contains_key("body"));
}

fn column_private_owner_is_part_of_the_payload_theorem(){

    assert_eq!(security::analyze(&b).invariants[0].direct_rule,Status::Proved);
}

    assert!(r.text().contains("non-failing"));assert!(r.text_with_all(true).contains("counterexample"));
}
